The class ranking Condition [paper (1)]: \(G_H\) dominates \(G_L\), and both supports lie at or above the reserve.
The two windows The high-cost window (H1), \(B_0(\pi)<c_H<B_1(M)\); the trading-cost window (H2), \(\Delta_0<k<\rho\kappa\Delta_1\).
The ceiling \(B(M(\pi))<c_H\), with \(g_H\ge g_L\) and \(\beta\le1/3\): no price recruits a high-cost challenger under any profile. (H3) is the ceiling at \(F_2\).
The entry ceiling \(r_C\approx3.592659\), where \(\tau(r)=M(1/2)\); the ceiling holds above it.
The informative branch The informative equilibrium that coexists with no trade: T at \(r_A\), R on \((r_A,r_F)\), then F; entry \(\rho+(1-\rho)\min\{b\Delta_T/(2k),b+\lambda/2\}\).
Coexistence No informed trade and an informative equilibrium at one strength; exactly \([r_A,r_N]\) under IM and C1. Not a mixture inside one equilibrium.

A 15-minute talk

  1. (1 min) Question. Does a stronger rival keep a costly entrant out? The standard answer is yes.

  2. (2 min) Setting. The decision interval; a prepared incumbent; a challenger that watches the price; one order slot (Figure 2).

  3. (3 min) Two claims on one surplus. \(\Delta_T=(r-\ell)^2/(2r)\) rises while \(B_r(\mu)\) falls; the split \(\Delta_T+g_H-g_L=h-\ell\) (Figure 3).

  4. (3 min) What one order reveals. The bounds \([m,M]\); price sufficiency; the residual advantage and the bounds \(\rho\kappa\Delta_T\le K_j\le\Delta_T\).

  5. (3 min) Main result. (H1)-(H3) in words; Table 7; entry rises, then returns to \(\rho\).

  6. (1 min) Controls. At fixed information entry cannot rise; frozen orders keep it flat; a hidden price keeps it at \(\rho\).

  7. (2 min) Path, research and scope. Coexistence and the informative branch (Figure 4); research rises with entry; access raises proceeds and surplus; the payment rule sets the sign; the purchase impact carries the entry component.

Appendices

Verification, parameters, and equation map

AIndependent verification of the numbers

The check uses the fixed manuscript snapshot c78e2c3 and its numbers.tex registry. The auction and cost primitives enter as exact fractions. The slot uses the declared \(\beta=0.294655\), with \(1+u=\mathrm{e}\). The chapter programs print the calculations beside their registry values. The separate program verify/A_crosscheck.py derives the headline values from these primitives. It imports no chapter program.

The independent auction calculation integrates payments and rents over the incumbent’s uniform law. It then constructs the order probabilities, posteriors, entry decisions and competitive prices. Their class averages give entry, ownership, target proceeds and surplus. Exact fractions also define the research equation and the equations for \(r_A\) and \(r_J\). Bisection uses exact signs for these roots; the other expressions use 220 decimal digits. This recomputation does not repeat the paper’s proofs of uniqueness or its exact Sturm counts.

For Euler’s number, let \(S=\sum_{n=0}^{110}1/n!\). The first omitted term and a geometric bound on the tail give \[S<\mathrm{e}<S+\frac{112}{111\cdot111!}.\] Both endpoints are exact fractions. The program uses their midpoint at high precision and prints the enclosure width. This enclosure is exact; the general calculations use high precision rather than interval certificates. For a decimal display, agreement means that the independent value rounds to the registry value. For a fraction, agreement means equality.

The runner saves each chapter’s output in verify/output/ and prints its exit code and run time. The cross-check saves its own output there too. It prints each independent value, its registry value and their difference. It also scans every chapter for typed decimals and reads the adjacent % verify/ source comment. The scan distinguishes literal matches, format differences, rounded matches and missing evidence. It lists layout decimals and paper references separately.

Result: What the check found

All 178 headline comparisons agree at the registry’s displayed precision. All eleven chapter programs exit with code zero. Every typed economic decimal has a source comment, and each agrees with its program after the stated display rounding. A matching output token proves its presence, not the validity of the associated argument.

Robustness

Cost laws

[paper Prop. A.4]. Calibration B with the cost law \(\rho H^0+(1-\rho)H^1\): \(H^0\) is any law on \([0,L]\), \(L=1/5\), and \(H^1\) is uniform on \([6-\varepsilon,6+\varepsilon]\) with \(\varepsilon=1/10\) or \(\varepsilon=1/1000\) (Section 11.1).

High prior \(1\) \(10\) \(7/10\) \(36/5\) \(1/100\) \(11/10\) \(3\) \(18/5\)
Wide classes \(U[3/5,7/5]\) \(U[1,19]\) \(7/10\) \(67/10\) \(1/50\) \(1\) \(9/2\) \(11/2\)
Same means \(1\) \(10\) \(7/10\) \(67/10\) \(1/50\) \(1\) \(9/2\) \(11/2\)

The same-means row is a control: point masses at the means of the wide classes, where a purchase does not recruit.

Many classes

[paper Prop. A.6].

  • Four classes. The slot, costs, reserve, \(r_0\) and \(r_1\) of B; equal priors \(1/4\); point values \(1\) and \(1001/1000\) (low group), \(9999/1000\) and \(10\) (high group).

  • The price collision. The slot of B; priors \((1/4,1/2,1/4)\); \(G_1=\delta_1\), \(G_2=(1-w)\delta_1+w\delta_{10}\), \(G_3=\delta_{10}\) with \(w=(1+2b_0+\lambda)/2=0.940709\); \(R\sim U[0,8]\), \(p=1\), \(\rho=1/4\), \(c_H=10\), \(k=1/8\).

(Section 11.3.)

Complementary signals

[paper Prop. A.7; App. A.9].

Vector Values
\(S(B)\) The slot, \(h\), \(\ell\), \(p\) and \(\pi\) of B; \(\rho=0.85\), \(c_H=7.14\), \(k=0.015\), \(r_0=1.1\), \(r_1=2.3\); accuracies \((a_T,d)=(0.70,0.75)\).
Exact witness \(S(B)\) with the slot \((\lambda,\beta)=(1/3,3/10)\), so \(b=1/5\), \(u=5/3\), \(m=3/11\), \(M=8/11\).
B with the accuracies of \(S(B)\) Calibration B with \((a_T,d)=(0.70,0.75)\).
B with its own accuracies Calibration B with \((a_T,d)=(0.94,0.505)\).

(Section 11.4.)

The HTML edition preserves the complete guide. Symbolic reasoning comes first; registry-backed benchmark values and interactive figures follow.

Sources and reproducibility

Every registry value links its display to the manuscript registry. Charts evaluate the paper's closed forms at those declarations. Mathematics is rendered locally with KaTeX. The page makes no network requests.
